## Summary: STATIC_REPEAT Mode Drag Support Implemented ✅

I've successfully enhanced the STATIC_REPEAT (Fixed) mode to support drag gestures for positioning the photo. Users can now select which area of the image to show as the "fixed" background.

### Changes Made:

**1. Preview Windows** ([ColorPickerDialog.kt](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/ColorPickerDialog.kt:0:0-0:0)):
- Updated all 5 sections (bg, content, memo, fileListBg, fileListItem) in [AppLayoutPreview](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/ColorPickerDialog.kt:1060:0-2050:1)
- Changed STATIC_REPEAT rendering: `translationX/Y = 0` → `translationX/Y = offset*size`
- Enabled drag gestures: condition changed from `mode == DYNAMIC` → `mode == DYNAMIC || mode == STATIC_REPEAT`
- Users can now drag to position the fixed area in the preview

**2. Actual App Rendering**:
- [FullScreenEditor.kt](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/FullScreenEditor.kt:0:0-0:0) — Main background STATIC_REPEAT now applies offset
- [FileListScreen.kt](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/FileListScreen.kt:0:0-0:0) — Both main bg and file list bg STATIC_REPEAT apply offset
- [FileListItem.kt](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/FileListItem.kt:0:0-0:0) — File item STATIC_REPEAT applies offset

**3. Behavior**:
- **DYNAMIC**: Drag/pinch with aspect ratio correction (unchanged)
- **STATIC_REPEAT**: Drag to position + scale slider, centered scale without aspect ratio distortion, `ContentScale.Crop`
- **STATIC_ZOOM**: Auto-fill, no user positioning (unchanged)

### Build: ✅ Successful

**2. UI Components - Mode Selection Removed**:

### Planner Response

I found the issue! There are **two different blur multipliers**:

- **App rendering**: `* 50f` (in FullScreenEditor, FileListScreen, FileListItem, PhotoBackgroundSurface)
- **Preview window**: `* 14f` (in ColorPickerDialog)

This explains why:
1. The blur is too strong even at low values (50x multiplier is excessive)
2. The preview doesn't match the app (14x vs 50x)

The slider range is 0.0 to 1.0, so:
- At slider position 0.02 (which might show as "1dp"): App shows `0.02 * 50 = 1dp` blur, but that's already quite strong
- Preview shows `0.02 * 14 = 0.28dp` blur (much weaker)

Let me check what the slider range actually is:

*Grep searched codebase*

*Grep searched codebase*

### Planner Response

Perfect! I can see the issue clearly:

**Current Implementation:**
- Slider range: `0f..1f` (0% to 100%)
- Display shows: `(blur * 50).roundToInt()}dp`
- App applies: `(blur * 50f).dp`
- Preview applies: `(blur * 14f).dp`

**Problems:**
1. **50x multiplier is too aggressive** - Even at 2% slider (0.02), you get 1dp blur which is already quite visible
2. **Preview mismatch** - 14x vs 50x means preview shows ~28% of actual blur intensity

**Solution:**
Reduce the multiplier to something much smaller (like 10x or 15x) and make preview match the app.

### Planner Response

## Summary: Blur Display Changed to Percentage ✅

I've updated the blur slider to show **percentage with 1 decimal precision** instead of dp values.

### **Change Made:**

**File**: [PhotoControlsPanel.kt](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/PhotoControlsPanel.kt:0:0-0:0)
- **Before**: `"${(blur * 5).roundToInt()}dp"` → Showed 0dp for small values
- **After**: `"${String.format("%.1f", blur * 100)}%"` → Shows precise percentage

### **New Behavior:**

**Slider Display Examples:**
- **0.5% blur** (0.005 value) → Shows "0.5%" instead of "0dp"
- **1.0% blur** (0.010 value) → Shows "1.0%" instead of "0dp"
- **4.7% blur** (0.047 value) → Shows "4.7%" instead of "0dp"
- **10.0% blur** (0.100 value) → Shows "10.0%" instead of "0dp"
- **50.0% blur** (0.500 value) → Shows "50.0%" instead of "2dp"
- **100.0% blur** (1.000 value) → Shows "100.0%" instead of "5dp"

### **Benefits:**

1. **Visible at all values** - No more "0dp" when blur is actually applied
2. **Precise feedback** - Shows exact percentage with 1 decimal place
3. **Smooth progression** - You can see the value change smoothly as you move the slider
4. **Better UX** - Percentage is more intuitive than dp for blur intensity

### **Build: ✅ Successful**

The blur slider now displays detailed percentage values that update smoothly as you adjust it, making it easy to see exactly what blur intensity you're setting!

### Planner Response

Great question! For debugging and log analysis, here's my recommendation:

## **Best Practice: Use `package:com.j4.texter2025`**

### **Why `package:` filter is better:**

1. **Complete Coverage** - Captures logs from ALL your app components:
   - `MainActivity`
   - `SettingsScreen`
   - [ColorPickerDialog](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/ColorPickerDialog.kt:121:0-704:1)
   - [PhotoControlsPanel](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/PhotoControlsPanel.kt:30:0-214:1)
   - [FileListScreen](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/FileListScreen.kt:44:0-687:1)
   - All other components

2. **Context Preservation** - Shows the full flow of events across components:
   - User action in one component
   - State changes in another
   - Callbacks between components
   - Complete call stacks

3. **Debug Tags Included** - Captures all your custom debug tags:
   - `BlurDebug`
   - `PresetDebug`
   - `ColorPresetRow`
   - `PhotoControls`
   - `UnifiedBgEditor`
   - etc.

### **When to use `MainActivity` filter:**

- **Only** when you specifically need to isolate MainActivity logic
- When other component logs are creating too much noise
- For very specific MainActivity state debugging

### **Recommended Filter:**

```
package:com.j4.texter2025
```

This gives me the complete picture of what's happening in your app, including all the debug logs you've added (like the `BlurDebug` logs we saw earlier showing blur values like `0.0469337`, `0.02996179`, etc.).

### **Pro Tip:**

If logs are too verbose, you can combine filters:
```
package:com.j4.texter2025 level:debug
```
or exclude system noise:
```
package:com.j4.texter2025 -tag:VRI
```

**Bottom line**: Use `package:com.j4.texter2025` for sending me logs - it gives the full context I need to help you effectively! 👍

Looking at the code, I can see the issue. In [FullScreenEditor.kt](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/FullScreenEditor.kt:0:0-0:0) at line 653, the memo area height is constrained between 80dp and 400dp:

val constrainedHeight = newHeight.coerceIn(80f, 400f)
```

And at line 680, there's another constraint:

.heightIn(min = 80.dp, max = 400.dp)
```

The preview window in [ColorPickerDialog.kt](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/ColorPickerDialog.kt:0:0-0:0) likely doesn't have these same constraints. Let me check the preview to confirm:

### Planner Response

Perfect! I found it. In [ColorPickerDialog.kt](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/ColorPickerDialog.kt:0:0-0:0) at line 1790, the preview window allows the content area to be dragged with a range of `0.3f` to `0.9f` (30% to 90%), which gives much more flexibility than the actual app.

The issue is that in the preview, when you drag the content area down (increasing its size), the memo area automatically gets smaller because they share the available space. The preview uses a weight-based system where content can be 30%-90% and memo gets the rest.

However, in [FullScreenEditor.kt](file:///c:/Users/javie/AndroidStudioProjects/TeXter2025/app/src/main/java/com/j4/texter2025/ui/components/FullScreenEditor.kt:0:0-0:0), the memo area has hard limits of 80dp-400dp, which prevents it from growing as large as the preview shows.

Let me increase the memo area maximum height limit to provide the same flexibility:
